<analysis>**original_problem_statement:**
The user initially wanted to build a comprehensive Christian music streaming app, Gracefy, and requested fixes for an existing mobile app.

After multiple failed attempts to fix persistent crashing and permission issues on the mobile app, the user became frustrated and requested to **completely delete the app and redevelop a new one from scratch.**

The new requirements for the rebuilt mobile app are:
*   A Spotify-like look and feel.
*   Connect to the existing backend database and admin panel.
*   **Home Screen:**
    *   Dynamic sections driven by the admin Layout Manager.
    *   Hero section (carousel) with content picked by the admin.
    *   Spotify-style Quick Access grids (including Liked Songs and user-created playlists) linked to the Layout Manager.
    *   A dedicated row/section for Bible and books.
    *   A section for Mafundisho na katekesi (Teachings and Catechesis) with a custom card design.
    *   A section for Mchanganyiko maalumu (Special Mixes).
    *   A section for Churches (Makanisa).
    *   Use a mix of row layouts (large horizontal rectangles, small squares, lists).
*   **Player:**
    *   An advanced audio player.
    *   The mini-player at the bottom should be hidden when the full-screen Now Playing view is open.
    *   The Now Playing screen should have Add to Playlist (), Share, and Download buttons.
*   **Features:**
    *   **Bible Section:** Implement Text-to-Speech (TTS) using the existing Google API to read verses in Swahili.
    *   **Add to Playlist:** A  button on songs should open a bottom sheet modal with options to Like song, Create new playlist, and add to existing playlists.
    *   **Artworks:** Songs in lists should display the album's thumbnail if they don't have their own.

**User's preferred language**: English (with Swahili terms for UI elements)

**what currently exists?**
The project consists of a FastAPI backend, a React admin panel, and a React Native mobile app.

The key development in this session was a **complete rebuild of the mobile app from scratch** to address persistent crashes and align with the user's new vision for a Spotify-like UI. The old mobile app codebase was deleted.

The new mobile app has been built with a dark theme and includes placeholder screens for Home, Search, Library, Album, Bible, and Churches. It is connected to the existing backend.

The backend  was significantly improved by replacing an incomplete data source with a full 66-book Swahili Bible JSON, and the data was successfully imported into the database.

**Last working item**:
    - Last item agent was working: The agent just completed a massive feature implementation push on the newly rebuilt mobile app. This included:
        1.  Integrating the Home Screen with the backend Layout Manager to dynamically render sections (Hero, Quick Access, Mafundisho, etc.).
        2.  Activating the Bible and Churches sections.
        3.  Implementing the Add to Playlist flow.
        4.  Fixing the mini-player visibility so it doesn't overlap with the full-screen player.
        5.  Adding album art fallbacks for songs.
        6.  Redesigning the Now Playing screen controls.
      A new build containing all these features was started and delivered to the user.
    - Status: USER VERIFICATION PENDING
    - Agent Testing Done: N
    - Which testing method agent to use? both
    - User Testing Done: N

**Code Architecture**


**Key Technical Concepts**
- **Application Rebuild from Scratch:** Responding to user frustration by deleting an entire codebase and rebuilding it based on new design principles (Spotify-like UI).
- **Dynamic UI from Backend CMS:** Linking the React Native Home Screen components to a  API endpoint, allowing an admin to control UI sections without an app update.
- **Expo Build Pipeline Debugging:** Diagnosing and resolving a wide range of  issues, including authentication tokens, account build quotas, missing dependencies, and corrupted files in the build context.
- **Data Source Migration:** Identifying an incomplete data source (Bible API) and replacing it by finding a new source, updating the backend service to parse the new format, and repopulating the database.
